total=weights.reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0);if(total<=0)throw Error('Positive total weight required');\n+ const norm=weights.map(w=>w/total),classes=probabilities[0].length;\n+ const return {scores,winner:scores.indexOf(Math.max(...scores))};\n+}\n+export function adaRound(weights,truth,prediction){\n+ const sum=weights.reduce((s,w)=>s+w,0),w=weights.map(w=>w/sum),error=w.reduce((s,v,i)=>s+(truth[i]!==prediction[i]?v:0),0);\n+ if(error===0)return {error,alpha:null,weights:w,status:'Clasificador perfecto: detener esta demostración.'};\n+ if(error>=.5)return {error,alpha:null,weights:w,status:'Sin ventaja binaria: rechazar o revisar el estimador; no continuar esta demostración.'};\n+ const alpha=.5*Math.log((1-error)/error),raw=w.map((v,i)=>v*Math.exp(-alpha*truth[i]*prediction[i])),z=raw.reduce((s,v)=>s+v,0);\n+ return {error,alpha,weights:raw.map(v=>v/z),status:'Los errores ganan peso   if(!best||loss<best.loss)best={split,left,right,fit,loss};\n+  }\n+  prediction=prediction.map((v,i)=>v+eta*best.fit[i]);history.push(mse(prediction));rounds.push({threshold:best.split-.5,left:best.left,right:best.right});\n+ }\n+ return     "D:\\GitHub\\ML NJIT\\web\\src\\week7Math.js": {
      "type": "add",
      "content": "export function ensembleVote(probabilities,weights,mode='soft'){\n const total=weights.reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0);if(total<=0)throw Error('Positive total weight required');\n const norm=weights.map(w=>w/total),classes=probabilities[0].length;\n const scores=Array.from({length:classes},(_,c)=>mode==='hard'?probabilities.reduce((s,p,i)=>s+(p.indexOf(Math.max(...p))===c?norm[i]:0),0):mode==='geometric'?Math.exp(probabilities.reduce((s,p,i)=>s+norm[i]*Math.log(Math.max(p[c],1e-15)),0)):probabilities.reduce((s,p,i)=>s+norm[i]*p[c],0));\n return {scores,winner:scores.indexOf(Math.max(...scores))};\n}\nexport function adaRound(weights,truth,prediction){\n const sum=weights.reduce((s,w)=>s+w,0),w=weights.map(w=>w/sum),error=w.reduce((s,v,i)=>s+(truth[i]!==prediction[i]?v:0),0);\n if(error===0)return {error,alpha:null,weights:w,status:'Clasificador perfecto: detener esta demostración.'};\n if(error>=.5)return {error,alpha:null,weights:w,status:'Sin ventaja binaria: rechazar o revisar el estimador; no continuar esta demostración.'};\n const alpha=.5*Math.log((1-error)/error),raw=w.map((v,i)=>v*Math.exp(-alpha*truth[i]*prediction[i])),z=raw.reduce((s,v)=>s+v,0);\n return {error,alpha,weights:raw.map(v=>v/z),status:'Los errores ganan peso relativo tras normalizar.'};\n}\nexport const boostY=[1,1.4,1.2,3.9,4.4,4.1,7.5,7.9];\nexport function residualBoost(stages,eta){\n const y=boostY,n=y.length,mean=y.reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)/n;let prediction=y.map(()=>mean);\n const mse=p=>y.reduce((s,v,i)=>s+(v-p[i])**2,0)/n,history=[mse(prediction)],rounds=[];\n for(let m=0;m<stages;m++){\n  const residual=y.map((v,i)=>v-prediction[i]);let best=null;\n  for(let split=1;split<n;split++){\n   const left=residual.slice(0,split).reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)/split,right=residual.slice(split).reduce((a,b)=>a+b,0)/(n-split),fit=residual.map((_,i)=>i<split?left:right),loss=residual.reduce((s,v,i)=>s+(v-fit[i])**2,0);\n   if(!best||loss<best.loss)best={split,left,right,fit,loss};\n  }\n  prediction=prediction.map((v,i)=>v+eta*best.fit[i]);history.push(mse(prediction));rounds.push({threshold:best.split-.5,left:best.left,right:best.right});\n }\n return {y,mean,prediction,history,rounds,residual:y.map((v,i)=>v-prediction[i])};\n}\n"
    },

Separate forecasting from diagnosis')\n+p('A reconstruction estimates unobserved pressure at the current time. A hydraulic emulator maps demands or operating conditions to pressure for those conditions. A temporal predictor anticipates a future reading. A healthy reference is deliberately designed to reveal deviations caused by leaks. These tasks may share inputs and models, but their outputs and performance measures are different.')\n+table([['Work','Task and contribution','Relation to our target'],['Garðarsson et al. With a four-row window, preprocessing averages the first three and targets the fourth.'],['Daniel et al., LILA [4]','Linear relations between contemporaneous pressures and flow inputs, followed by change detection and hydraulic localization.','Useful contrast: predicting a reference pressure to detect leaks is not the same objective as following recorded future pressure.'],['Zhang and Fink, AIGNN [5]','Algorithm-informed graph embeddings transferred to pressure reconstruction/prediction for leak diagnosis.','Topology and temporal pressure prediction already have substantial antecedents.']], [98,228,178])\n+p('The distinction matters during a continuing leak. A forecast of the pressure actually observed may adapt to the changed regime, while a healthy reference may preserve a residual to diagnose the anomaly. These are legitimate but different objectives. Our report will not present detection scores as forecasting error.')\n+h('Published results and their limits')\n+p('Garðarsson et al. report 19 detected events, 138 false positives and four false negatives in their diagnostic evaluation [2]. The selected repository is pinned to commit <font name=\"Courier\">864a99041af6</font> [6].')\n+p('The inspected data loader aligns the last historical input with the next target. For our horizon, an online update could use a label only after the corresponding future measurement arrives. The selected LILA snapshot is pinned to <font name=\"Courier\">b488a60db197</font> [8].')\n+h('Course connections and additional techniques')\n+p('The solutions extend regression, kNN, loss functions and model evaluation covered in DS675. Adding one of these familiar models alone would not demonstrate a new contribution.')\n+h('Reproducibility boundary')\n+p('Our evidence base contains 46 PDF files and 11 selected code snapshots, including multiple versions and auxiliary studies. The review includes static code/data checks, not reproduction of third-party training. Original SCADA measurements are separated from newly simulated L-Town scenarios and from studies on other networks. Results in this draft remain attributed to their authors.')\n+story.append(PageBreak())\n+\n+title('A candidate extension and evaluation')\n+h('4. Contribution to define and test')\n+p('We propose to investigate selection or use of measurements according to both their hydraulic relation to the target and the age of the last available reading. The concrete question is whether a hydraulically related but stale measurement is more useful than a less related, fresh one for observed pressure at +15 minutes. The selection rule is not yet specified or claimed to be novel worldwide.')\n+p('This preserves Milestone 1 while going beyond simply changing the horizon or adding Random Forest. The rule must be contrasted with prior sensor-selection and delayed-information methods. The partially verified AGN forecasting work also needs its target and horizons resolved before making stronger novelty statements.')\n+table([['Comparison','Purpose'],['Persistence and causal seasonal reference','Test whether learned forecasts improve beyond pressure inertia and time patterns'],['Regularized regression/VAR; optionally trees or boosting','Compare model capacity under the same available information'],['Correlation-selected subset vs hydraulic subset','Separate physical context from statistical selection at equal sensor budget'],['Proposed age-aware selection; full inputs','Quantify incremental value and the cost of more measurements']], [220,284])\n+p('All alternatives must share target, horizon, lag policy, tuning budget and information availability. Sensor count, derived feature count and number of lags will be reported separately. If the target sensor is delayed, persistence must also use its last received reading, rather than a fresher value hidden from the learned model.')\n+h('Prospective evaluation protocol')\n+p('Use 2018 for sensor selection, training and temporal validation; reserve 2019 for final evaluation after decisions are frozen. Purge origins whose +15-minute labels cross a training boundary. Fit scales and learned feature selection only in permitted training data. No centered interpolation or future demand/control measurement may enter the predictors.')\n+p('Start with unperturbed official data. A later experiment may impose reading ages of 0, 5, 10 or 15 minutes; these are simulated delays because actual communication latency is not recorded. Preserve the original pressure labels and evaluate MAE/RMSE in metres, skill versus persistence, sensor budget and errors by predeclared time/regime groups. Leak truth may describe evaluation regimes, not model inputs or test-driven selection.')\n+story.append(PageBreak())\n+\n+title('Discussion and linked references')\n+h('5. Topology, sparse sensors, drift adaptation and robustness already have antecedents. What the reviewed corpus does not establish as a solved comparison is our specific official-SCADA +15-minute experiment with equal information budgets and separate treatment of reading age. This is a gap in the reviewed evidence, not proof of worldwide priority.')\n+p('If the proposed rule is too close to an existing method, a coherent alternative is prediction intervals with empirically assessed coverage and width across regimes. Delayed-label adaptation is another option, but SAM-kNN and SICAMS must be acknowledged. Leak alarms from forecast residuals would change the project objective and require event-based false-alarm/delay evaluation.')\n+p('Before submission, specify the sensor and rule, complete the overlapping literature, check any quantitative result retained, refine this draft and verify the final 3-5 page PDF. Contribution to define and test')\np('We propose to investigate selection or use of measurements according to both their hydraulic relation to the target and the age of the last available reading. The concrete question is whether a hydraulically related but stale measurement is more useful than a less related, fresh one for observed pressure at +15 minutes. The selection rule is not yet specified or claimed to be novel worldwide.')\np('This preserves Milestone 1 while going beyond simply changing the horizon or adding Random Forest. The rule must be contrasted with prior sensor-selection and delayed-information methods. The partially verified AGN forecasting work also needs its target and horizons resolved before making stronger novelty statements.')\ntable([['Comparison','Purpose'],['Persistence and causal seasonal reference','Test whether learned forecasts improve beyond pressure inertia and time patterns'],['Regularized regression/VAR; optionally trees or boosting','Compare model capacity under the same available information'],['Correlation-selected subset vs hydraulic subset','Separate physical context from statistical selection at equal sensor budget'],['Proposed age-aware selection; full inputs','Quantify incremental value and the cost of more measurements']], [220,284])\np('All alternatives must share target, horizon, lag policy, tuning budget and information availability. Sensor count, derived feature count and number of lags will be reported separately. If the target sensor is delayed, persistence must also use its last received reading, rather than a fresher value hidden from the learned model.')\nh('Prospective evaluation protocol')\np('Use 2018 for sensor selection, training and temporal validation; reserve 2019 for final evaluation after decisions are frozen. Purge origins whose +15-minute labels cross a training boundary. Fit scales and learned feature selection only in permitted training data. No centered interpolation or future demand/control measurement may enter the predictors.')\np('Start with unperturbed official data. A later experiment may impose reading ages of 0, 5, 10 or 15 minutes; these are simulated delays because actual communication latency is not recorded. Preserve the original pressure labels and evaluate MAE/RMSE in metres, skill versus persistence, sensor budget and errors by predeclared time/regime groups. Leak truth may describe evaluation regimes, not model inputs or test-driven selection.')\nstory.append(PageBreak())\n\ntitle('Discussion and linked references')\nh('5. Gaps, limitations and next decisions')\np('The reviewed literature establishes pressure reconstruction, healthy references, leak diagnosis and some next-step temporal prediction. Topology, sparse sensors, drift adaptation and robustness already have antecedents. What the reviewed corpus does not establish as a solved comparison is our specific official-SCADA +15-minute experiment with equal information budgets and separate treatment of reading age. This is a gap in the reviewed evidence, not proof of worldwide priority.')\np('If the proposed rule is too close to an existing method, a coherent alternative is prediction intervals with empirically assessed coverage and width across regimes. Delayed-label adaptation is another option, but SAM-kNN and SICAMS must be acknowledged. Leak alarms from forecast residuals would change the project objective and require event-based false-alarm/delay evaluation.')\np('Before submission, specify the sensor and rule, complete the overlapping literature, check any quantitative result retained, refine this draft and verify the final 3-5 page PDF. No improvement over persistence is promised before experiments. Abrupt exogenous leak onset may not have an observable precursor.')\nh('References and selected code')\nrefs=[\n ('[1] Vrachimis et al. Los quince notebooks de la carpeta cuentan con inventario de estructura, títulos y hashes; no se afirma que se hayan ejecutado completos los originales.\n+\n+## Contenido añadido\n+\n+Ocho lecciones: diversidad; hard/soft/geometric voting; bagging; pesos de ejemplos/votos; ronda AdaBoost; AdaBoost en API; residuos de gradient boosting; evaluación de ensembles. Los enlaces del portal llevan a su ficha de fuente; los originales se conservan localmente.\n+\n+## Hallazgos y precisiones\n+\n+- Hard voting multiclase puede elegir por pluralidad, sin mayoría estricta. Soft voting requiere orden de clases alineado y no garantiza calibración.\n+- Bootstrap puede hacer n extracciones con reemplazo; no exige n'<n. Bagging con atributos por modelo y Random Forest con candidatos por split se distinguen.\n+- AdaBoost usa error ponderado por la distribución actual de ejemplos. 1}\\\\par\\r\\n    \\\\vspace{0.4mm}\\r\\n    {\\\\color{white!88}\\\\large Proposed Datasets for Pressure Prediction}\\r\\n  \\\\end{minipage}%\\r\\n  \\\\begin{minipage}[c]{0.34\\\\linewidth}\\r\\n    \\\\raggedleft\\r\\n    {\\\\color{white}\\\\bfseries DS675}\\\\par\\r\\n    {\\\\color{white!92}\\\\small Andres Arango Martinez}\\\\par\\r\\n    {\\\\color{Gold}\\\\scriptsize\\\\bfseries NJIT ID: 31748996 \\\\enspace | \\\\enspace UCID: aa3978}\\r\\n  \\\\end{minipage}\\r\\n\\\\end{tcolorbox}\\r\\n\\r\\n\\\\vspace{0.9mm}\\r\\n\\\\begin{contextbox}\\r\\n\\\\small\\r\\n\\\\labeltext{Area of interest and selection rationale.}\\r\\nMy research interests include pressure management in drinking-water distribution systems, particularly pressure behavior at remote critical nodes. I have previously worked on this problem using field measurements and model-based control methods, as described in a \\\\href{https://ascelibrary.org/doi/10.1061/JWRMD5.WRENG-7321}{\\\\textit{published case study}}. For this mini-project, I would like to study whether supervised machine learning can use current and historical SCADA measurements to forecast future pressure at a monitored node. The assignment asks for Kaggle datasets with usability above 9. BATADAL satisfies that criterion with a Kaggle usability of 10.0. BattLeDIM currently shows 3.53 on Kaggle, so it does not meet that specific threshold; I retain it as a candidate because its original source has substantial academic support, including a peer-reviewed ASCE benchmark paper, an official research archive, and EPANET network models.\\r\\n\\\\end{contextbox}\\r\\n\\r\\n\\\\vspace{1.0mm}\\r\\n{\\\\color{Navy}\\\\bfseries\\\\large Candidates at a glance}\\\\par\\r\\n\\\\vspace{0.5mm}\\r\\n\\\\footnotesize\\r\\n\\\\rowcolors{2}{PaleBlue}{white}\\r\\n\\\\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\\\\bfseries\\\\color{Navy}\\\\raggedright\\\\arraybackslash}m{1.10in}>{\\\\raggedright\\\\arraybackslash}m{2.89in}>{\\\\raggedright\\\\arraybackslash}m{2.89in}@{}}\\r\\n  \\\\rowcolor{Blue!13}\\r\\n  & \\\\multicolumn{1}{c}{\\\\textbf{BattLeDIM: L-Town}} & \\\\multicolumn{1}{c}{\\\\textbf{BATADAL: C-Town}} \\\\\\\\\\r\\n  Kaggle usability & 3.53 (below the assignment threshold) & 10.0 (meets the assignment threshold) \\\\\\\\\\r\\n  Kaggle link & \\\\href{https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/larserikrisholm/battledim}{Dataset page} & \\\\href{https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/minhbtnguyen/batadal-a-dataset-for-cyber-attack-detection}{Dataset page} \\\\\\\\\\r\\n  Sampling & 5 minutes & 1 hour \\\\\\\\\\r\\n  Records & 210,240 observations across 2018--2019 & 15,027 observations across three files \\\\\\\\\\r\\n  Main variables & 33 pressures, 3 flows, 1 tank level, 82 AMR demands & 12 pressures, 12 flows, 12 actuator states, 7 tank levels \\\\\\\\\\r\\n  Proposed task & Pressure 15 minutes ahead; supervised regression & Pressure 1 hour ahead; supervised regression \\\\\\\\\\r\\n\\\\end{tabular}\\r\\n\\\\normalsize\\r\\n\\r\\n\\\\vspace{1.5mm}\\r\\n\\\\begin{datasetbox}[Teal]{Candidate 1 \\\\quad BattLeDIM: L-Town}\\r\\n\\\\small\\r\\n\\\\textbf{Dataset characteristics and academic provenance.}\\r\\nBattLeDIM is a simulated SCADA benchmark for L-Town, a city-scale drinking-water network inspired by a real system in Cyprus. Its Kaggle copy has a usability rating of 3.53, which is below the assignment's requested value of greater than 9. I am including it transparently because the underlying dataset is not an informal Kaggle-only collection: it was developed for an international leakage-detection benchmark, documented in a peer-reviewed \\\\textit{Journal of Water Resources Planning and Management} paper, and released with an official Zenodo archive and EPANET network models. The benchmark paper describes 782 junctions and 905 pipe segments and divides the network into three hydraulic areas. The SCADA data contain 105,120 five-minute observations in each of 2018 and 2019, for 210,240 observations total. The main measurement table has 119 variables: 33 pressures, three flows, one tank level, and 82 automated meter reading (AMR) demand measurements. Leakage-event information is also available from the original benchmark for later diagnostic analysis.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\\\vspace{1.1mm}\\r\\n\\\\textbf{Proposed predictive-learning task.}\\r\\nI would use BattLeDIM for supervised regression: predict the recorded pressure head at one monitored node 15 minutes into the future. Predictors would be limited to information available at forecast time, such as current and lagged pressures, flows, tank level, AMR demands, and calendar features. I would select the target sensor after exploratory analysis of topology and pressure variability rather than labeling a node ``critical'' in advance. Because 119 candidate variables may be unnecessary, I would compare an all-network baseline with a topology-informed subset drawn from the same hydraulic area and its boundary connections. Leakage periods could later be used to evaluate whether forecast errors change under abnormal hydraulic conditions.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\\\vspace{0.9mm}\\r\\n\\\\scriptsize\\r\\n\\\\labeltext{Sources:} \\\\href{https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/larserikrisholm/battledim}{Kaggle} \\\\sepmark\\r\\n\\\\href{https://zenodo.org/records/4017659}{Official Zenodo archive} \\\\sepmark\\r\\n\\\\href{https://doi.org/10.1061/(ASCE)WR.1943-5452.0001601}{Vrachimis et al.
